By Newton
Luke 8:16﻿–﻿21.
I
“My barns are full, my stores increase,
 

 And now, for many years,
 

 Soul, eat and drink, and take thine ease,
 

 Secure from wants and fears.”
II
Thus while a worldling boasted once,
 

 As many now presume,
 

 He heard the Lord himself pronounce
 

 His sudden awful doom.
III
“This night, vain fool, thy soul must pass
 

 Into a world unknown;
 

 And who shall then the stores possess
 

 Which thou hast call’d thine own?”
IV
Thus blinded mortals fondly scheme
 

 For happiness below,
 

 Till death disturbs the pleasing dream,
 

 And they awake to woe.
V
Ah! who can speak the vast dismay
 

 That fills the sinner’s mind,
 

 When, torn by Death’s strong hand away,
 

 He leaves his all behind.
VI
Wretches, who cleave to earthly things,
 

 But are not rich to God,
 

 Their dying hour is full of stings,
 

 And hell their dark abode.
VII
Dear Saviour, make us timely wise
 

 Thy gospel to attend,
 

 That we may live above the skies
 

 When this poor life shall end.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 8:16–21。
一
「我的仓房满盈，我的储存加增，
如今，为着许多年，
灵魂啊，吃吧喝吧，安享自在，
稳脱缺乏与恐惧。」
二
当一个世界之子这样夸口，
如今许多人也这样放胆，
他听见主亲自宣告
他突临的可怖厄运。
三
「今夜，愚妄的人，你的灵魂必过去，
进入一个未知的世界；
那时谁将拥有
你所称为你自己的储存？」
四
这样，瞎眼的必死者为
下面的幸福天真筹划，
直到死亡搅扰那美梦，
他们便醒入祸患。
五
啊！谁能述说那充满
罪人心中的巨大惊惶，
当他被死亡的大手撕去，
撇下他的一切。
六
那些紧抓属地之物、
却不富足归神的可怜人，
他们的临终之刻满刺，
地狱是他们的黑暗住处。
七
亲爱的救主，求你使我们及时智慧，
留意你的福音，
使我们在诸天之上活着，
当这可怜的生命终结。

## 103 The Barren Fig-Tree / 103 不结果的无花果树

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 13:6﻿–﻿9.
I
The church a garden is,
 

 In which believers stand
 

 Like ornamental trees
 

 Planted by God’s own hand!
 

 His Spirit waters all their roots,
 

 And every branch abounds with fruits.
II
But other trees there are
 

 In this enclosure grow,
 

 Which, though they promise fair,
 

 Have only leaves to show:
 

 No fruits of grace are on them found,
 

 They stand but cumberers of the ground.
III
The under gardener grieves,
 

 In vain his strength he spends,
 

 For heaps of useless leaves
 

 Afford him small amends:
 

 He hears the Lord his will make known,
 

 To cut the barren fig-trees down.
IV
How difficult his post,
 

 What pangs his bowels move,
 

 To find his wishes cross’d,
 

 His labours useless prove!
 

 His last relief is earnest prayer,
 

 “Lord, spare them yet another year.
V
“Spare them, and let me try
 

 What further means may do;
 

 I’ll fresh manure apply,
 

 My digging I’ll renew:
 

 Who knows but yet they fruit may yield?
 

 If not﻿—’tis just, they must be fell’d.”
VI
If under means of grace
 

 No gracious fruits appear,
 

 It is a dreadful case,
 

 Though God may long forbear:
 

 At length he’ll strike the threaten’d blow,12
 

 And lay the barren fig-tree low.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 13:6–9。
一
教会是一个园子，
信徒在其中站立
如装饰的树木，
由神亲手栽种！
他的圣灵浇灌它们所有的根，
每条枝子果实丰盛。
二
但在这围子里
还长着别的树，
虽然它们前景美好，
却只有叶子可显示：
其上寻不见恩典的果子，
它们只是白占地土。
三
园丁忧伤，
徒然花费他的力量，
因为成堆无用的叶子
给他的补偿微少：
他听见主表明他的旨意，
要砍下不结果的无花果树。
四
他的岗位何等艰难，
他的肺腑何等疼痛，
发现他的愿望受挫，
他的劳苦归于无用！
他最后的安慰是恳切的祷告：
「主啊，今年且留着它们。
五
「且留着它们，让我试试
更多的方法能作什么；
我要施上新肥，
我要重新掘土：
谁知它们或许还会结果？
若不——那公义要求它们被砍倒。」
六
若在蒙恩之道下
不显出恩典的果子，
那是可怕的境况，
虽然神可能长久宽容：
最终他必挥出那威吓的一击，12
放倒那不结果的无花果树。

## 104 The Prodigal Son / 104 浪子

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 15:11﻿–﻿24.
I
Afflictions, though they seem severe,
 

 In mercy oft are sent;
 

 They stopp’d the prodigal’s career,
 

 And forced him to repent.
II
Although he no relentings felt
 

 Till he had spent his store,
 

 His stubborn heart began to melt
 

 When famine pinch’d him sore.
III
“What have I gain’d by sin,” he said,
 

 “But hunger, shame, and fear?
 

 My father’s house abounds with bread
 

 While I am starving here.
IV
I’ll go and tell him all I’ve done,
 

 And fall before his face;
 

 Unworthy to be call’d his son,
 

 I’ll seek a servant’s place.”
V
His father saw him coming back,
 

 He saw and ran and smiled,
 

 And threw his arms around the neck
 

 Of his rebellious child.
VI
“Father, I’ve sinn’d﻿—but O forgive!”
 

 “I’ve heard enough,” he said;
 

 “Rejoice, my house﻿—my son’s alive
 

 For whom I mourn’d as dead.
VII
“Now let the fatted calf be slain,
 

 And spread the news around:
 

 My son was dead, but lives again﻿—
 

 Was lost, but now is found.
VIII
’Tis thus the Lord his love reveals
 

 To call poor sinners home;
 

 More than a father’s love he feels.
 

 And welcomes all that come.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 15:11–24。
一
患难虽然看似严厉，
却常在怜悯中差来；
它们止住了浪子的奔途，
强制他悔改。
二
虽然他不感悔恨，
直到花尽了储存，
他那顽固的心开始熔化，
当饥荒重压着他。
三
「我从罪得了什么，」他说，
「除了饥饿、羞耻与恐惧？
我父家粮食丰盈，
我却在这里挨饿。
四
我要回去向他承认一切所作，
俯伏在他面前；
不配称为他的儿子，
我要寻求仆人的位置。」
五
他父亲见他回来，
他看见，便跑去微笑，
把双臂环绕
那悖逆孩子的颈项。
六
「父亲，我犯了罪——哦，赦免！」
「我听够了，」他说；
「欢乐吧，我的家——我的儿子活了，
我为他哀恸如死。
七
「现在宰杀肥牛犊，
把消息传遍四围：
我儿子死了，却又活了——
失丧了，如今寻回了。」
八
主就这样启示他的爱，
呼召可怜的罪人回家；
他感受的过于父亲的爱，
并欢迎一切来的人。

## 105 The Rich Man and Lazarus / 105 财主与拉撒路

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 16:19﻿–﻿25.
I
A worlding spent each day
 

 In luxury and state,
 

 While a believer lay
 

 A beggar at his gate.
 

 Think not the Lord’s appointment strange,
 

 Death made a great and lasting change.
II
Death brought the saint release
 

 From want, disease, and scorn;
 

 And to the land of peace
 

 His soul, by angels borne,
 

 In Abraham’s bosom safely placed,
 

 Enjoys an everlasting feast.
III
The rich man also died,
 

 And in a moment fell
 

 From all his pomp and pride
 

 Into the flames of hell:
 

 The beggar’s bliss, from far beheld,
 

 His soul with double anguish fill’d.
IV
“O, Abra’m, send,” he cries,
 

 (But his request was vain,)
 

 “The beggar from the skies
 

 To mitigate my pain!
 

 One drop of water I entreat,
 

 To sooth my tongue’s tormenting heat.”
V
Let all who worldly pelf
 

 And worldly spirits have
 

 Observe, each for himself,
 

 The answer Abra’m gave:
 

 “Remember thou wast fill’d with good,
 

 While the poor beggar pined for good.
VI
“Neglected at thy door,
 

 With tears he begg’d his bread;
 

 But now he weeps no more﻿—
 

 His griefs and pains are fled:
 

 His joys eternally will flow,
 

 While thine expire in endless woe.”
VII
Lord, make us truly wise,
 

 To choose thy people’s lot,
 

 And earthly joys despise,
 

 Which soon will be forgot:
 

 The greatest evil we can fear,
 

 Is to possess our portion here!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 16:19–25。
一
一个世界之子天天
度奢华尊贵的生活，
而一个信徒躺着，
作他门前的乞丐。
不要以为主的安排奇怪，
死亡带来了巨大持久的改变。
二
死亡带给圣徒释放，
脱离匮乏、疾病与轻蔑；
他的灵魂被天使接去，
到平安之地，
安然安置在亚伯拉罕的怀里，
享受永远的筵席。
三
那财主也死了，
霎时从他一切的
浮华与骄傲
坠入地狱的火焰：
远远望见乞丐的福乐，
使他的灵魂充满双重的苦楚。
四
「哦，亚伯拉罕，求你差，」他喊，
（但他的请求徒然，）
「从天上差那乞丐
来减轻我的痛！
我求一滴水，
舒缓我舌燃烧的苦。」
五
愿一切拥有属世钱财
与属世之灵的人，
各为自己留心
亚伯拉罕的回答：
「要记念你从前享美物，
而那可怜的乞丐渴慕美物。
六
「他被撇在你门口，
流泪乞讨他的饼；
但如今他不再哭泣——
他的忧伤与疼痛已逃遁：
他的喜乐必永远涌流，
而你的在无尽的祸患中断绝。」
七
主啊，使我们真有智慧，
选择你百姓的份，
并轻看属世的宴乐，
它们不久必被忘却：
我们所能惧怕的最大邪恶，
是在此地拥有我们的份！

## 106 The Importunate Widow.13 / 106 切求的寡妇.13

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 18:1﻿–﻿7.
I
Our Lord, who knows full well
 

 The heart of every saint,
 

 Invites us, by a parable.
 

 To pray and never faint.
II
He bows his gracious ear,
 

 We never plead in vain;
 

 Yet we must wait till he appear,
 

 And pray, and pray again.
III
Though unbelief suggest,
 

 Why should we longer wait?
 

 He bids us never give him rest,
 

 But be importunate.
IV
’Twas thus a widow poor,
 

 Without support or friend,
 

 Beset the unjust judge’s door,
 

 And gain’d, at last, her end.
V
For her he little cared,
 

 As little for the laws;
 

 Nor god nor man did he regard,
 

 Yet he espoused her cause.
VI
She urged him day and night,
 

 Would no denial take;
 

 At length he said, “I’ll do her right,
 

 For my own quiet’s sake.”
VII
And shall not Jesus hear
 

 His chosen when they cry?
 

 Yes, though he may a while forbear,
 

 He’ll help them from on high.
VIII
His nature, truth, and love,
 

 Engage him on their side;
 

 When they are grieved, his bowels move,
 

 And can they be denied?
IX
Then let us earnest be,
 

 And never faint in prayer;
 

 He loves our importunity,
 

 And makes our cause his care.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 18:1–7。
一
我们的主，他全然知道
每个圣徒的心，
借一个比喻邀请我们，
祷告而永不懈怠。
二
他垂下恩慈的耳，
我们从未恳求徒然；
但我们必等候直到他显现，
并祷告，再祷告。
三
虽然不信暗示，
为何还要再等候？
他吩咐我们决不容他安息，
而要切求不舍。
四
就这样一个穷寡妇，
无依靠无朋友，
缠着那不义法官的门，
最终达到了她的目的。
五
他对她少有关怀，
对法律也少有尊重；
他既不敬神也不顾人，
却站在她的事业一边。
六
她日夜催促他，
不接受拒绝；
最后他说：「我要为她伸冤，
为我自己的安宁。」
七
耶稣岂不垂听
他选民的呼求吗？
是的，他虽可能一时宽容延迟，
必从高处帮助他们。
八
他的性情、信实与爱，
都站在他们一边；
当他们忧伤，他的肺腑被激动，
他们岂会被拒绝？
九
那么让我们恳切，
在祷告中永不懈怠；
他爱我们的切求，
并以我们的事为念。

## 107 Zaccheus / 107 撒该

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 19:1﻿–﻿6.
I
Zaccheus climb’d the tree,
 

 And thought himself unknown;
 

 But how surprised was he
 

 When Jesus call’d him down!
 

 The Lord beheld him, though conceal’d,
 

 And by a word his power reveal’d.
II
Wonder and joy at once
 

 Were painted in his face;
 

 “Does he my name pronounce,
 

 And does he know my case?
 

 Will Jesus deign with me to dine?
 

 Lord, I, with all I have, are thine.”
III
Thus, where the gospel’s preach’d,
 

 And sinners come to hear,
 

 The hearts of some are reach’d
 

 Before they are aware:
 

 The word directly speaks to them,
 

 And seems to point them out by name.
IV
’Tis curiosity
 

 Oft brings them in the way,
 

 Only the man to see
 

 And hear what he can say;
 

 But how the sinner starts to find
 

 The preacher knows his inmost mind!
V
His long-forgotten faults
 

 Are brought again in view,
 

 And all his secrets thoughts
 

 Reveal’d in public too.
 

 Though compass’d with a crowd about,
 

 The searching word has found him out.
VI
While thus distressing pain
 

 And sorrow fill his heart,
 

 He hears a voice again,
 

 That bids his fears depart,
 

 Then like Zaccheus he is blest,
 

 And Jesus deigns to be his guest.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 19:1–6。
一
撒该爬上树，
以为自己不被知道；
但当他被耶稣唤下时，
他是何等惊奇！
主看见他，虽然他隐藏，
借一句话启示了他的大能。
二
惊奇与喜乐一时
绘在他的脸上；
「他竟说出我的名，
他知道我的案情吗？
耶稣竟屈尊与我同席？
主啊，我和我一切所有的都是你的。」
三
这样，在传讲福音之处，
罪人们来听道，
有些人的心被摸着，
在他们未察觉之前：
这道直接对他们说话，
似按名把他们指出来。
四
是好奇心
常把他们带进这路，
只为见那个人
并听他能说什么；
但罪人何等惊起，
发现传道者知道他内心最深处！
五
他久被遗忘的过犯
再被摆在眼前，
他一切隐秘的思想
也在公众面前显露。
虽然被人群四围，
那鉴察的道已把他寻出。
六
当这样伤痛
与忧愁充满他的心，
他再听见一个声音，
吩咐他的恐惧离去，
那时他像撒该一样蒙福，
耶稣屈尊作他的客人。

## 108 The Believer’s Danger, Safety, and Duty / 108 信徒的危险、安全与本分

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 22:31﻿–﻿32.
I
“Simon beware,” the Saviour said﻿—
 

 Satan, your subtle foe,
 

 Already has his measures laid
 

 Your soul to overthrow.
II
“He wants to sift you all as wheat,
 

 And thinks his vict’ry sure;
 

 But I his malice will defeat﻿—
 

 My pray’r shall faith secure.”
III
Believers, tremble and rejoice,
 

 Your help and danger view;
 

 This warning has to you a voice,
 

 This promise speaks to you.
IV
Satan beholds with jealous eye
 

 Your privilege and joy;
 

 He’s always watchful, always nigh,
 

 To tear and to destroy.
V
But Jesus lives to intercede,
 

 That faith may still prevail;
 

 He will support in time of need,
 

 And Satan’s art shall fail.
VI
Yet let us not the warning slight,
 

 But watchful still be found;
 

 Though faith cannot be slain in fight,
 

 It may receive a wound.
VII
While Satan watches dare we sleep?
 

 We must our guard maintain;
 

 But, Lord, do thou the city keep,
 

 Or else we watch in vain.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 22:31–32。
一
「西门，要儆醒，」救主说——
撒但，你们狡猾的仇敌，
已经设下他的计谋
要倾覆你们的灵魂。
二
「他想要筛你们如筛麦子，
以为他的胜利确定；
但我必挫败他的恶毒——
我的祷告必保守信心。」
三
信徒们，要战兢又要喜乐，
看你们的帮助与危险；
这警告对你们有声音，
这应许对你们说话。
四
撒但以嫉妒的眼
观看你们的特权与喜乐；
他总是儆醒，总在近旁，
要撕裂要毁坏。
五
但耶稣活着代求，
使信心仍可得胜；
他必在需要之时扶持，
撒但的诡计必失败。
六
然而不要轻看这警告，
仍要被寻得儆醒；
信心虽不能在争战中被杀，
它可能受一个伤。
七
撒但守望，我们竟敢睡觉吗？
我们必须持守信卫；
但主啊，愿你保守这城，
不然我们守望徒然。

## 109 Father, Forgive Them / 109 父啊，赦免他们

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 23:34.
I
“Father, forgive,” the Saviour said,
 

 “They know not what they do:”
 

 His heart was moved when thus he pray’d
 

 For me, my friends, and you.
II
He saw that as the Jews abused
 

 And crucified his flesh,
 

 So he by us would be refused,
 

 And crucified afresh.
III
Through love of sin we long were prone
 

 To act as Satan bid;
 

 But now with grief and shame we own
 

 We knew not what we did.
IV
We knew not the desert of sin,
 

 Nor whom we thus defied;
 

 Nor where our guilty souls had been
 

 If Jesus had not died.
V
We knew not what a law we broke,
 

 How holy, just, and pure!
 

 Nor what a God we durst provoke,
 

 But thought ourselves secure.
VI
But Jesus all our guilt foresaw,
 

 And shed his precious blood,
 

 To satisfy the holy law,
 

 And make our peace with God.
VII
My sin, dear Saviour, made thee bleed,
 

 Yet didst thou pray for me!
 

 I knew not what I did, indeed,
 

 When ignorant of thee.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 23:34。
一
「父啊，赦免，」救主说，
「他们所作的他们不晓得：」
他这样祷告时心被感动，
为我、我的朋友和你。
二
他看见犹太人怎样苦害
并钉他的肉体，
他也照样被我们拒绝，
并被重新钉十字架。
三
因对罪的爱，我们长久倾向
照撒但所吩咐的行事；
但如今我们怀着忧伤羞愧承认，
我们所作的我们不晓得。
四
我们不知罪当受的报应，
也不知我们所触犯的是谁；
也不知我们有罪的灵魂
若耶稣没有死，会在哪里。
五
我们不知所犯的是何等的律法，
何等圣洁、公义、纯洁！
也不知我们所敢触怒的是何等的神，
却自以为安稳。
六
但耶稣预见了我们一切的罪咎，
流了他宝贵的血，
以满足圣洁的律法，
并使我们与神和好。
七
我的罪，亲爱的救主，使你流血，
你却还为我祷告！
当我还不认识你时，
我所作的我实在不晓得。

## 110 The Two Malefactors / 110 两个罪犯

===Original===
By Newton
Luke 23:39﻿–﻿43.
I
Sovereign grace has power alone
 

 To subdue a heart of stone;
 

 And the moment grace is felt,
 

 Then the hardest heart will melt.
II
When our Lord was crucified
 

 Two transgressors with him died!
 

 One, with vile blaspheming tongue,
 

 Scoff’d at Jesus as he hung.
III
Thus he spent his wicked breath
 

 In the very jaws of death;
 

 Perish’d, as too many do,
 

 With the Saviour in his view.
IV
But the other, touch’d with grace,
 

 Saw the danger of his case﻿—
 

 Faith received to own the Lord,
 

 Whom the scribes and priests abhorr’d.
V
“Lord,” he pray’d, “remember me
 

 When in glory thou shalt be!”
 

 “Soon with me,” the Lord replies,
 

 “Thou shalt rest in paradise.”
VI
This was wondrous grace indeed,
 

 Grace vouchsafed in time of need!
 

 Sinners, trust in Jesus’ name,
 

 You shall find him still the same.
VII
But beware of unbelief,
 

 Think upon the harden’d thief;
 

 If the gospel you disdain,
 

 Christ to you will die in vain.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
路加福音 23:39–43。
一
主权恩典惟独有大能，
制伏石头的心；
恩典一被感受，
最硬的心也必熔化。
二
当我们的主被钉十字架，
两个犯人与他同死！
一个用卑劣亵渎的舌，
讥诮悬挂的耶稣。
三
他就这样在死亡的颚中
耗尽他邪恶的气息；
像太多的人那样灭亡，
救主还在他眼前。
四
但另一个被恩典摸着，
看见他案情的危险——
蒙赐信心承认主，
就是文士和祭司所憎恶的。
五
「主啊，」他祷告，「求你记念我，
当你进入荣耀之时！」
「今日就同我，」主回答，
「你必安息在乐园里。」
六
这确是奇异的恩典，
在需要之时所赐的恩典！
罪人们，要信靠耶稣的名，
你们必寻见他仍是一样。
七
但要谨防不信，
要思想那刚硬的强盗；
你若轻看福音，
基督向你就是徒然死了。

## 111 The Woman of Samaria / 111 撒玛利亚妇人

===Original===
By Newton
John 4:28.
I
Jesus, to what didst thou submit,
 

 To save thy dear-bought flock from hell!
 

 Like a poor trav’ler see him sit
 

 Athirst and weary by the well.
II
The woman who for water came
 

 (What great events on small depend!)
 

 Then learn’d the glory of his name,
 

 The well of life, the sinner’s Friend!
III
Taught from her birth to hate the Jews,
 

 And fill’d with party pride, at first
 

 Her zeal induced her to refuse
 

 Water, to quench the Saviour’s thirst.
IV
But soon she knew the gift of God,
 

 And Jesus, whom she scorn’d before,
 

 Unask’d, that drink on her bestow’d
 

 Which whoso tastes shall thirst no more.
V
His words her prejudice removed,
 

 Her sin she felt, relief she found;
 

 She saw and heard, believed and loved,
 

 And ran to tell her neighbours round.
VI
O come, this wondrous man behold,
 

 The promised Saviour! this is he
 

 Whom ancient prophecies foretold,
 

 Born from our guilt to set us free,
VII
Like her, in ignorance content,
 

 I worshipp’d long I knew not what;
 

 Like her, on other things intent,
 

 I found him when I sought him not.
VIII
He told me all that e’er I did,
 

 And told me all was pardon’d too;
 

 And now, like her, as he has bid,
 

 I live to point him out to you.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约翰福音 4:28。
一
耶稣，你甘愿忍受什么，
为救你重价买来的羊脱离地狱！
看他像个可怜的旅客
干渴疲乏地坐在井旁。
二
那来打水的妇人
（何等大事系于小事！）
那时得知他名的荣耀，
生命之井，罪人的朋友！
三
她从出生被教导恨犹太人，
满有党派的骄傲，起初
她的热心使她拒绝
给水，解救主的渴。
四
但她不久知道神的恩赐，
和耶稣，那她从前轻看的，
未被求问，就赐她那饮品，
凡尝的必永远不渴。
五
他的话除去她的偏见，
她感受她的罪，寻得解救；
她看见又听见，相信又爱，
便跑去告诉四围的邻舍。
六
哦，来看这奇妙的人，
那应许的救主！这就是他，
古时预言所预言的，
生来为使我们脱离罪疚，
七
像她，满足于无知，
我长久敬拜我不知的什么；
像她，心思在别的事上，
我不寻求他时寻见了他。
八
他把我所行的一切告诉了我，
又告诉我一切都蒙赦免；
如今像她，照他所吩咐的，
我活着把你指向他。

## 112 The Pool of Bethesda.14 / 112 毕士大池.14

===Original===
By Newton
John 5:2﻿–﻿4.
I
Beside the gospel pool,
 

 Appointed for the poor,
 

 From year to year my helpless soul
 

 Has waited for a cure.
II
How often have I seen
 

 The healing waters move,
 

 And others, round me, stepping in,
 

 Their efficacy prove!
III
But my complaints remain:
 

 I feel the very same;
 

 As full of guilt and fear and pain
 

 As when at first I came.
IV
O would the Lord appear
 

 My malady to heal;
 

 He knows how long I’ve languish’d here,
 

 And what distress I feel.
V
How often have I thought,
 

 Why should I longer lie?
 

 Surely the mercy I have sought
 

 Is not for such as I?
VI
But whether can I go?
 

 There is no other pool
 

 Where streams of sovereign virtue flow
 

 To make a sinner whole.
VII
Here, then, from day to day,
 

 I’ll wait and hope and try;
 

 Can Jesus hear a sinner pray,
 

 Yet suffer him to die!
VIII
No: he is full of grace;
 

 He never will permit
 

 A soul that fain would see his face
 

 To perish at his feet.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约翰福音 5:2–4。
一
在福音的池旁，
为穷人指定的，
年复一年我无助的灵魂
等候着医治。
二
我何等多次看见
医治的水流动，
别人，在我四围，踏进去，
证明它们的功效！
三
但我的病症仍在：
我感受一模一样；
照样满有罪咎、恐惧与疼痛，
像我初来之时。
四
哦，愿主显现
医治我的疾病；
他知道我在此憔悴多久，
我所感受的苦楚。
五
我何等多次想，
为何还要再躺卧？
我所寻求的怜悯
 surely不是为像我这样的人？
六
但我能往哪里去？
没有别的池子
流出主权大能的溪流
使罪人完全。
七
那么在这里，日复一日，
我要等候、盼望、尝试；
耶稣岂能听罪人祷告，
却容他死亡！
八
不：他满有恩典；
他永不容许
一个愿见他面的灵魂
在他脚前灭亡。

## 113 Another / 113 另一首

===Original===
By Newton
I
Here at Bethesda’s pool, the poor,
 

 The wither’d, halt, and blind,
 

 With waiting hearts expect a cure,
 

 And free admittance find.
II
Here streams of wondrous virtue flow,
 

 To heal the sin-sick soul﻿—
 

 To wash the filthy white as snow,
 

 And make the wounded whole.
III
The dumb break forth in songs of praise,
 

 The blind their sight receive,
 

 The cripple runs in wisdom’s ways,
 

 The dead revive and live.
IV
Restrain’d to no one case or time,
 

 These waters always move;
 

 Sinners in every age and clime
 

 Their vital influence prove.
V
Yet numbers daily near them lie,
 

 Who meet with no relief;
 

 With life in view they pine and die
 

 In hopeless unbelief.
VI
’Tis strange they should refuse to bathe.
 

 And yet frequent the pool;
 

 But none can even wish for faith,
 

 While love of sin bears rule.
VII
Satan their consciences has seal’d
 

 And stupifies their thought;
 

 For were they willing to be heal’d,
 

 The cure would soon be wrought.
VIII
Do thou, dear Saviour, interpose,
 

 Their stubborn wills constrain;
 

 Or else to them the water flows
 

 And grace is preached in vain.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
在毕士大池这里，穷人、
枯干的、瘸腿的与瞎眼的，
怀着等候的心期待医治，
寻得白白的进入。
二
这里流出奇妙大能的溪流，
医治患罪病的灵魂——
把污秽的洗得白如雪，
使受伤的完全。
三
哑巴开口发出赞美，
瞎眼的重见光明，
瘸腿的奔走智慧之路，
死人复苏存活。
四
不限于一事或一时，
这些水总是搅动；
每个时代每个地域的罪人
证明它们赋予生命的影响。
五
然而多人天天躺在其近旁，
却未得解救；
望着生命憔悴而死，
在无望的不信中。
六
奇怪他们竟拒绝下水，
却仍常来池边；
但无人能盼求信心，
当对罪的爱掌权时。
七
撒但封了他们的良心，
使他们思想麻木；
因为他们若愿意得医治，
那医治早必作成。
八
亲爱的救主，求你介入，
强制他们顽固的意志；
否则对他们水就白流，
恩典也徒然传讲。

## 114 The Disciples at Sea.15 / 114 海上的门徒.15

===Original===
By Newton
John 6:16﻿–﻿21.
I
Constrain’d by their Lord to embark,
 

 And venture without him to sea,
 

 The season tempestuous and dark,
 

 How grieved the disciples must be!
 

 But though he remain’d on the shore,
 

 He spent the night for them in pray’r;
 

 They still were as safe as before,
 

 And equally under his care.
II
They strove, though in vain, for awhile
 

 The force of the waves to withstand,
 

 But when they were wearied with toil
 

 They saw their dear Saviour at hand:
 

 They gladly received him on board,
 

 His presence their spirits revived,
 

 The sea became calm at his word,
 

 And soon at their port they arrived.
III
We, like the disciples, are toss’d,
 

 By storms on a perilous deep;
 

 But cannot be possibly lost,
 

 For Jesus has charge of the ship:
 

 Though billows and winds are enraged,
 

 And threaten to make us their sport;
 

 This pilot his word has engaged
 

 To bring us in safety to port.
IV
If sometimes we struggle alone,
 

 And he is withdrawn from our view,
 

 It makes us more willing to own
 

 We nothing without him can do:
 

 Then Satan our hopes would assail,
 

 But Jesus is still within call,
 

 And when our poor efforts quite fail
 

 He comes in good time and does all.
V
Yet, Lord, we are ready to shrink
 

 Unless we thy presence perceive;
 

 save us, (we cry,) or we sink;
 

 We would, but we cannot believe!
 

 The night has been long and severe,
 

 The winds and the seas are still high,
 

 Dear Saviour, this moment appear,
 

 And say to our souls, “It is I!”16
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约翰福音 6:16–21。
一
受他们的主约束上船，
没有他而冒险出海，
季节风暴又黑暗，
门徒们何等忧伤！
但他虽留在岸上，
却为他们彻夜祷告；
他们仍如从前安全，
同样在他的看顾之下。
二
他们一时徒然挣扎，
要抵挡波浪的势力，
但当他们疲于劳碌，
便见亲爱的救主在手边：
他们欣然接他上船，
他的同在复苏他们的灵，
海因他的话平静，
他们不久到达口岸。
三
我们像门徒，被抛掷，
在危险深处的风暴中；
但不可能失丧，
因耶稣掌管这船：
虽然波涛与狂风忿怒，
威胁要把我们作玩物；
这位舵手已以他的话承担
要安领我们进港。
四
我们若有时独自挣扎，
他从我们眼前退隐，
这使我们更愿意承认
没有他我们一无所能：
撒但那时要攻击我们的盼望，
但耶稣仍在呼召之内，
当我们可怜的努力尽告失败，
他及时而来成就一切。
五
然而主啊，我们易退缩，
除非我们感知你的同在；
「求你拯救，」（我们喊，）「不然我们沉没；
我们愿意，却不能相信！
黑夜漫长而严酷，
风与海仍然高企，
亲爱的救主，此刻显现，
对我们的灵魂说：「是我！」16

## 115 Will Ye Also Go Away? / 115 你们也要去吗？

===Original===
By Newton
John 6:67﻿–﻿69.
I
When any turn from Zion’s way﻿—
 

 Alas! what numbers do!﻿—
 

 Methinks I hear my Saviour say,
 

 “Wilt thou forsake me too?”
II
Ah, Lord! with such a heart as mine,
 

 Unless thou hold me fast,
 

 I feel I must, I shall decline,
 

 And prove like them at last.
III
Yet thou alone hast power, I know,
 

 To save a wretch like me;
 

 To whom, or whither, could I go,
 

 If I should turn from thee?
IV
Beyond a doubt I rest assured
 

 Thou art the Christ of God,
 

 Who hast eternal life secured
 

 By promise and by blood.
V
The help of men and angels join’d
 

 Could never reach my case;
 

 Nor can I hope relief to find
 

 But in thy boundless grace.
VI
No voice but thine can give me rest,
 

 And bid my fears depart;
 

 No love but thine can make me bless’d.
 

 And satisfy my heart.
VII
What anguish has that question stirr’d,
 

 If I will also go?
 

 Yet, Lord, relying on thy word,
 

 I humbly answer﻿—No.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约翰福音 6:67–69。
一
当有人转离锡安的路——
唉！这样的人何等多！——
我想我听见我救主说：
「你也要离我而去吗？」
二
啊，主啊！凭我这样的心，
你若不紧紧持住我，
我知我必须、也必要退后，
最终证明像他们一样。
三
然而惟独你有大能，我知道，
能拯救像我这样的可怜人；
我能向谁去，或往哪里去，
我若转离你？
四
毫无疑惑我安息确信
你是神的基督，
借应许和宝血
确保了永生。
五
人与天使的帮助联合
也永不能及我的境况；
我也不能盼望寻得解救，
除非在你无限的恩典里。
六
惟独你的声音能赐我安息，
吩咐我的恐惧离去；
惟独你的爱能使我有福，
并满足我的心。
七
那问题何等搅动忧伤：
我是否也要去？
然而主啊，倚靠你的话，
我谦卑回答——不。

## 116 The Resurrection and the Life / 116 复活与生命

===Original===
By Newton
John 11:25.
I
“I am,” saith Christ, “your glorious head,”
 

 (May we attention give,)
 

 “The resurrection of the dead,
 

 The life of all that live.
II
“By faith in me the soul receives
 

 New life, though dead before;
 

 And he that in my name believes,
 

 Shall live to die no more.
III
“The sinner, sleeping in his grave,
 

 Shall at my voice awake;
 

 And when I once begin to save,
 

 My work I ne’er forsake.”
IV
Fulfill thy promise, gracious Lord,
 

 On us assembled here;
 

 Put forth thy Spirit with the word,
 

 And cause the dead to hear.
V
Preserve the power of faith alive
 

 In those who love thy name;
 

 For sin and Satan daily strive
 

 To quench the sacred flame.
VI
Thy power and mercy first prevail’d
 

 From death to set us free;
 

 And often since our life had fail’d,
 

 If not renew’d by thee.
VII
To thee we look, to thee we bow,
 

 To thee for help we call;
 

 Our life and resurrection thou,
 

 Our hope, our joy, our all.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约翰福音 11:25。
一
「我是，」基督说，「你们荣耀的元首，
（愿我们留意听，）
「死人复活，
一切活着者的生命。
二
「凭信我，灵魂领受
新的生命，虽然从前死了；
凡奉我名相信的，
必活着不再死。
三
「睡在坟墓中的罪人，
必因我的声音醒来；
我一旦开始拯救，
我的工作永不丢弃。」
四
求你成就你的应许，恩慈的主，
在我们聚集于此的人身上；
将你的圣灵与道一同差出，
叫死人听见。
五
保守信心的大能存活
在那些爱你名的人里面；
因罪与撒但天天力争
要熄灭那圣洁的火焰。
六
你的大能与怜悯首先得胜，
释放我们脱离死亡；
此后我们的生命屡次必已断绝，
若非被你更新。
七
我们仰望你，向你屈身，
向你呼求帮助；
你是我们的生命与复活，
我们的盼望、我们的喜乐、我们的一切。

## 117 Weeping Mary / 117 哭泣的马利亚

===Original===
By Newton
John 20:11﻿–﻿16.
I
Mary to her Saviour’s tomb
 

 Hasted at the early dawn;
 

 Spice she brought, and sweet perfume;
 

 But the Lord she loved was gone.
 

 For awhile she weeping stood,
 

 Struck with sorrow and surprise,
 

 Shedding tears, a plenteous flood,
 

 For her heart supplied her eyes.
II
Jesus, who is always near,
 

 Though too often unperceived,
 

 Came, his drooping child to cheer,
 

 Kindly asking, “Why she grieved?”
 

 Though at first she knew him not,
 

 When he call’d her by her name
 

 Then her griefs were all forgot,
 

 For she found he was the same.
III
Grief and sighing quickly fled
 

 When she heard his welcome voice:
 

 Just before she thought him dead,
 

 Now he bids her heart rejoice.
 

 What a change his word can make,
 

 Turning darkness into day!
 

 You who weep for Jesu’s sake,
 

 He will wipe your tears away.
IV
He who came to comfort her,
 

 When she thought her all was lost,
 

 Will for your relief appear,
 

 Though you now are tempest-toss’d:
 

 On his word your burden cast,
 

 On his love your thoughts employ;
 

 Weeping for awhile may last,
 

 But the morning brings the joy.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
约翰福音 20:11–16。
一
马利亚在清晨破晓时
急往她救主的坟墓；
她带着香料和香膏；
但她所爱的主已去了。
她一时流泪站立，
被忧伤与惊奇击中，
倾出泪水，丰沛如洪，
因她的心供应她的眼。
二
耶稣，他总在近旁，
虽然常未被察觉，
来鼓舞他下垂的孩子，
恩慈地问：「她为何忧伤？」
虽然起先她不认得他，
当他唤她的名，
那时她的忧伤尽被忘记，
因她发现他是同一位。
三
忧伤与叹息速速逃遁，
当她听见他受欢迎的声音：
片刻之前她以为他死了，
如今他吩咐她的心喜乐。
他的话能带来何等改变，
把黑暗变为白昼！
你们为耶稣的缘故哭泣的，
他必擦去你们的眼泪。
四
那位来安慰她的，
当她以为失去一切，
必为你们的解救显现，
虽然你们如今被风暴颠簸：
把你的重担卸在他的话上，
把你的思想用于他的爱；
哭泣可能持续一时，
但清晨必带来喜乐。

## 118 Lovest Thou Me? / 118 你爱我吗？

===Original===
By Cowper
John 21:16.
I
Hark, my soul! it is the Lord;
 

 ’Tis thy Saviour, hear his word;
 

 Jesus speaks, and speaks to thee:
 

 “Say, poor sinner, lov’st thou me?
II
“I deliver’d thee when bound,
 

 And, when bleeding, heal’d thy wound﻿—
 

 Sought thee wand’ring, set thee right,
 

 Turn’d thy darkness into light.
III
“Can a woman’s tender care
 

 Cease towards the child she bare?
 

 Yes, she may forgetful be,
 

 Yet will I remember thee.
IV
“Mine is an unchanging love,
 

 Higher than the heights above,
 

 Deeper than the depths beneath,
 

 Free and faithful, strong as death.
V
“Thou shalt see my glory soon,
 

 When the work of grace is done﻿—
 

 Partner of my throne shalt be,
 

 Say, poor sinner, lov’st thou me?”
VI
Lord, it is my chief complaint,
 

 That my love is weak and faint;
 

 Yet I love thee and adore,
 

 Oh for grace to love thee more?
===Chinese===
库珀 作
约翰福音 21:16。
一
听啊，我的灵魂！这是主；
是你的救主，听他的话；
耶稣说话，且对你说：
「说，可怜的罪人，你爱我吗？
二
「你被捆时我释放你，
你流血时我医治你的伤——
寻见流离的你，使你归正，
把你的黑暗变为光明。
三
「妇人的温柔看顾
岂能向她所生的孩子止息？
是的，她可能忘怀，
我却必记念你。
四
「我的爱是不改变的爱，
高过诸天的高度，
深过深渊的深处，
自由信实，坚强如死。
五
「你不久必见我的荣耀，
当恩典之工完成——
你必作我宝座的同享者，
说，可怜的罪人，你爱我吗？」
六
主啊，这是我主要的抱怨，
我的爱软弱又微弱；
然而我爱你，我敬拜你，
哦，愿恩典使我爱你更多？

## 119 Another / 119 另一首

===Original===
By Newton
I
’Tis a point I long to know,
 

 Oft it causes anxious thought﻿—
 

 Do I love the Lord, or no?
 

 Am I his, or am I not?
II
If I love, why am I thus?
 

 Why this dull, this lifeless frame?
 

 Hardly sure can they be worse
 

 Who have never heard his name!
III
Could my heart so hard remain,
 

 Pray’r a task and burden prove,
 

 Every trifle give me pain,
 

 If I knew a Saviour’s love?
IV
When I turn my eyes within,
 

 All is dark and vain and wild:
 

 Fill’d with unbelief and sin,
 

 Can I deem myself a child?
V
If I pray or hear or read,
 

 Sin is mix’d with all I do;
 

 You that love the Lord indeed,
 

 Tell me, is it thus with you?
VI
Yet I mourn my stubborn will,
 

 Find my sin a grief and thrall:
 

 Should I grieve for what I feel,
 

 If I did not love at all?
VII
Could I joy his saints to meet,
 

 Choose the ways I once abhorr’d,
 

 Find, at times, the promise sweet,
 

 If I did not love the Lord?
VIII
Lord, decide the doubtful case!
 

 Thou who art thy people’s sun,
 

 Shine upon thy work of grace,
 

 If it be indeed begun.
IX
Let me love thee more and more,
 

 If I love at all, I pray;
 

 If I have not loved before,
 

 Help me to begin to-day.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
一
这是我一直想知道的一点，
它常引起焦虑的思想——
我爱主吗，还是不爱？
我是属他的，还是不属？
二
我若爱，为何我这样？
为何这样迟钝、这样无生气？
他们 surely不能更糟，
那些从未听见他名的人！
三
我的心岂能这样刚硬，
祷告成为苦差重担，
每个琐事都使我疼痛，
我若知道救主的爱？
四
当我转眼向内，
一切黑暗、虚妄又狂野：
满有不信与罪，
我能自认为是孩子吗？
五
我若祷告、听道或阅读，
罪与我所作的一切相混；
你们真实爱主的人，
告诉我，你们也是这样吗？
六
然而我哀恸我顽固的意志，
发现我的罪是忧伤与捆绑：
我岂会为我的感受忧伤，
我若全然不爱？
七
我岂能喜与圣徒相聚，
选择我曾厌恶的路，
不时寻见应许甘甜，
我若不爱主？
八
主啊，求你解明这疑难的案子！
你是你子民的太阳，
照耀你恩典的工作，
它若真已开始。
九
让我爱你更多又更多，
我若全然爱，我祷告；
我若从前不曾爱，
帮助我今天开始。

## 120 The Death of Stephen / 120 司提反之死

===Original===
By Newton
Acts 7:54﻿–﻿60.
I
As some tall rock amidst the waves
 

 The fury of the tempest braves,
 

 While the fierce billows, tossing high,
 

 Break at its foot, and murm’ring, die:
II
Thus they who in the Lord confide,
 

 Though foes assault on every side,
 

 Cannot be moved or overthrown,
 

 For Jesus makes their cause his own.
III
So faithful Stephen, undismay’d,
 

 The malice of the Jews survey’d;
 

 The holy joy which fill’d his breast,
 

 A lustre on his face impress’d.
IV
“Behold,” he said, “the world of light
 

 Is open’d to my strengthened sight;
 

 My glorious Lord appears in view,
 

 That Jesus whom ye lately slew.”
V
With such a friend and witness near,
 

 No form of death could make him fear;
 

 Calm, amidst showers of stones, he kneels,
 

 And only for his murderers feels.
VI
May we by faith perceive thee thus,
 

 Dear Saviour, ever near to us!
 

 This sight our peace through life shall keep,
 

 And death be fear’d no more than sleep.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
使徒行传 7:54–60。
一
如波涛中一块高岩，
抵挡风暴的狂怒，
当猛浪高高抛掷，
在它脚前破碎、低鸣着消逝：
二
这样，那些倚靠主的人，
虽然仇敌四面攻击，
不能被摇动或倾覆，
因耶稣认他们的事业为他自己的。
三
忠信的司提反就这样毫无惧色，
察看犹太人的恶毒；
充满他胸中的圣洁喜乐，
把一层光辉印在他面上。
四
「看哪，」他说，「光的世界
向我加强的视力敞开；
我荣耀的主显在眼前，
就是你们最近所杀的那位耶稣。」
五
有这样一位朋友与见证在近旁，
没有任何死亡的形式能使他惧怕；
在石雨中他平静跪下，
只为他杀害他的人代求。
六
愿我们凭信心这样感知你，
亲爱的救主，总在我们近旁！
这景象必保守我们一生的平安，
死亡不再可怕，不过如睡眠。

## 121 The Rebel’s Surrender to Grace.﻿–﻿Lord, What Wilt Thou Have Me to Do? / 121 悖逆者向恩典降服。——主啊，我当作什么？

===Original===
By Newton
Acts 9:6.
I
Lord, thou hast won﻿—at length I yield﻿—
 

 My heart, by mighty grace compell’d,
 

 Surrenders all to thee;
 

 Against thy terrors long I strove,
 

 But who can stand against thy love?
 

 Love conquers even me.
II
All that a wretch could do I tried﻿—
 

 Thy patience scorn’d, thy power defied,
 

 And trampled on thy laws:
 

 Scarcely thy martyrs at the stake
 

 Could stand more steadfast for thy sake
 

 Than I in Satan’s cause.
III
But since thou hast thy love reveal’d,
 

 And shown my soul a pardon seal’d,
 

 I can resist no more:
 

 Couldst thou for such a sinner bleed?
 

 Canst thou for such a rebel plead?
 

 I wonder and adore!
IV
If thou hadst bid thy thunders roll,
 

 And lightnings flash, to blast my soul,
 

 I still had stubborn been;
 

 But mercy has my heart subdued﻿—
 

 A bleeding Saviour I have view’d,
 

 And now I hate my sin.
V
Now, Lord, I would be thine alone;
 

 Come, take possession of thine own,
 

 For thou hast set me free:
 

 Released from Satan’s hard command,
 

 See all my powers waiting stand
 

 To be employed by thee.
VI
My will conform’d to thine would move;
 

 On thee, my hope, desire, and love,
 

 In fix’d attention join;
 

 My hands, my eyes, my ears, my tongue,
 

 Have Satan’s servants been too long,
 

 But now they shall be thine.
VII
And can I be the very same
 

 Who lately durst blaspheme thy name,
 

 And on thy gospel tread?
 

 Surely each one who hears my case
 

 Will praise thee and confess thy grace
 

 Invincible indeed!
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
使徒行传 9:6。
一
主啊，你已得胜——我终于降服——
我的心，被大能的恩典强制，
向你交出一切；
我长久抵挡你的威吓，
但谁能抵挡你的爱？
爱甚至征服了我。
二
一个可怜人所能作的我尽都试过——
藐视你的忍耐，违抗你的大能，
并践踏你的律法：
你的殉道者在火刑柱上
几乎不能比我在撒但的事业中
站得更坚定。
三
但你既启示了你的爱，
又给我灵魂看盖印的赦免，
我不能再抗拒：
你能为这样的罪人流血吗？
你能为这样的悖逆者代求吗？
我惊奇并敬拜！
四
你若吩咐雷声滚动，
闪电飞射，毁灭我的灵魂，
我仍会顽固；
但怜悯制伏了我的心——
我看见了一位流血的救主，
如今我恨我的罪。
五
如今主啊，我愿单属你；
来，认领你自己的，
因你已释放我：
脱离撒但的严酷辖制，
看我一切的能力侍立等候
被你所用。
六
我的意志顺从你的旨意而动；
在你身上，我的盼望、渴慕与爱，
以固定的专注联合；
我的手、我的眼、我的耳、我的舌，
作撒但的仆役已经太久，
如今它们必属你。
七
我岂能仍是那同一个
最近还敢亵渎你名、
践踏你福音的人？
听我案情的人必都
赞美你，并承认你的恩典
确是无可抗拒！

## 122 Peter Released from Prison / 122 彼得出监

===Original===
By Newton
Acts 12:5﻿–﻿8.
I
Fervent persevering prayers
 

 Are faith’s assured resource;
 

 Brazen gates and iron bars
 

 In vain withstand their force:
 

 Peter, when in prison cast,
 

 Though by soldiers kept with care,
 

 Though the doors were bolted fast,
 

 Was soon released by prayer.
II
While he slept, an angel came,
 

 And spread a light around,
 

 Touch’d and call’d him by his name,
 

 And raised him from the ground:
 

 All his chains and fetters burst,
 

 Every door wide open flew;
 

 Peter thought he dream’d at first,
 

 But found the vision true.
III
Thus the Lord can make a way
 

 To bring his saints relief;
 

 ’Tis their part to wait and pray
 

 In spite of unbelief:
 

 He can break through walls of stone,
 

 Sink the mountain to a plain;
 

 They to whom his name is known
 

 Can never pray in vain.
IV
Thus in chains of guilt and sin,
 

 Poor sinners sleeping lie;
 

 No alarm is felt within,
 

 Although condemn’d to die,
 

 Till, descending from above,
 

 (Mercy smiling in his eyes,)
 

 Jesus, with a voice of love,
 

 Awakes, and bids them rise.
V
Glad the summons they obey,
 

 And liberty desire:
 

 Straight their fetters melt away
 

 Like wax before the fire:
 

 By the word of Him who died,
 

 Guilty pris’ners to release,
 

 Every door flies open wide,
 

 And they depart in peace.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
使徒行传 12:5–8。
一
恳切恒忍的祷告
是信心确实的资源；
铜门与铁闸
徒然抵挡它们的力量：
彼得被下在监里，
虽然被兵丁小心看守，
虽然门都紧闩，
不久便借祷告获释。
二
当他睡着，一位天使来，
四围散布光芒，
触摸他，唤他的名，
把他从地上扶起：
他一切的锁链镣铐断落，
每扇门大大飞开；
彼得起初以为自己做梦，
却发现异象真实。
三
这样，主能开出一条路，
带给他的圣徒解救；
他们的本分是等候并祷告，
不顾不信：
他能穿破石墙，
把大山沉为平原；
认识他名的人
永不能祷告徒然。
四
这样，在罪咎与罪的锁链中，
可怜的罪人睡着躺着；
里面不感警讯，
虽然被定死罪，
直到他从上降临，
（怜悯在他眼中微笑，）
耶稣以爱的声音，
唤醒他们，吩咐他们起来。
五
他们欣然顺从传唤，
渴望自由：
他们的镣铐立时熔化
如火前的蜡：
借那位死了、为释放
有罪囚犯者的言语，
每扇门大大飞开，
他们平安离去。

## 123 The Trembling Gaoler / 123 战兢的狱卒

===Original===
By Newton
Acts 16:29﻿–﻿31.
I
A believer, free from care,
 

 May in chains or dungeons sing,
 

 If the Lord be with him there,
 

 And be happier than a king:
 

 Paul and Silas thus confined,
 

 Though their backs were torn by whips,
 

 Yet, possessing peace of mind,
 

 Sung his praise with joyful lips.
II
Suddenly the prison shook,
 

 Open flew the iron doors;
 

 And the gaoler, terror-struck,
 

 Now his captives’ help implores:
 

 Trembling at their feet he fell,
 

 “Tell me, sirs, what must I do
 

 To be saved from guilt and hell?
 

 None can tell me this but you.”
III
“Look to Jesus,” they replied,
 

 “If on him thou canst believe,
 

 By the death which he has died,
 

 Thou salvation shalt receive.”
 

 While the living word he heard,
 

 Faith sprung up within his heart,
 

 And released from all he fear’d,
 

 In their joy his soul had part.
IV
Sinners, Christ is still the same;
 

 O that you could likewise fear!
 

 Then the mention of his name
 

 Would be music to your ear:
 

 Jesus rescues Satan’s slaves,
 

 His dear wounds still plead, “Forgive!”
 

 Jesus to the utmost saves;
 

 Sinners, look to him and live.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
使徒行传 16:29–31。
一
一个脱去挂虑的信徒，
能在锁链或地牢中歌唱，
若主与他同在，
比君王更快乐：
保罗和西拉这样被囚，
虽然他们的背被鞭撕裂，
却怀着心中的平安，
以喜乐的唇唱他的赞美。
二
忽然监狱震动，
铁门飞开；
那狱卒惊惶失措，
此刻恳求他囚犯的帮助：
他战兢地俯伏在他们脚前：
「先生们，告诉我，我当作什么
才能脱离罪咎与地狱？
除了你们无人能告诉我。」
三
「仰望耶稣，」他们回答，
「你若能信他，
借他所受的死，
必领受救恩。」
当他听见活道，
信心在他心中升起，
脱离他所怕的一切，
在他的喜乐中有份。
四
罪人们，基督仍是一样；
哦，愿你们也能这样敬畏！
那时他名的提说
必成为你耳中的音乐：
耶稣拯救撒但的奴仆，
他亲爱的伤仍恳求：「赦免！」
耶稣拯救到底；
罪人们，仰望他而活。

## 124 The Exorcists / 124 驱魔者

===Original===
By Newton
Acts 19:13﻿–﻿16.
I
When the apostle wonders wrought,
 

 And heal’d the sick in Jesu’s name,
 

 The sons of Sceva vainly thought
 

 That they had power to do the same.
II
On one possess’d they tried their art,
 

 And naming Jesus preach’d by Paul,
 

 They charged the spirit to depart,
 

 Expecting he’d obey their call.
III
The spirit answer’d with a mock,
 

 “Jesus I know, and Paul I know;
 

 I must have gone if Paul had spoke;
 

 But who are ye that bid me go?”
IV
With fury then the man he fill’d,
 

 Who on the poor pretenders flew;
 

 Naked and wounded, almost kill’d,
 

 They fled in all the people’s view.
V
Jesus! that name pronounced by faith,
 

 Is full of wonder-working power:
 

 It conquers Satan, sin, and death,
 

 And cheers in trouble’s darkest hour.
VI
But they who are not born again
 

 Know nothing of it but the sound;
 

 They do but take his name in vain
 

 When most their zeal and pains abound.
VII
Satan their vain attempts derides,
 

 Whether they talk or pray or preach;
 

 Long as the love of sin abides,
 

 His power is safe beyond their reach.
VIII
But you, believers, may rejoice,
 

 Satan well knows your mighty Friend;
 

 He trembles at your Saviour’s voice,
 

 And owns he cannot gain his end.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
使徒行传 19:13–16。
一
当使徒行神迹，
奉耶稣的名医治病人，
士基瓦的儿子们徒然以为
他们有能力行同样的事。
二
对一个被附的人他们施用伎俩，
奉保罗所传的耶稣之名，
吩咐那灵出去，
指望他会听从他们的呼召。
三
那灵带着讥诮回答：
「耶稣我认识，保罗我也知道；
保罗若开口我早走了；
但你们是谁，竟吩咐我走？」
四
于是他使那人充满狂怒，
向那可怜的冒充者扑去；
赤身负伤，几乎丧命，
他们在众民眼前逃走。
五
耶稣！凭信心念出的这名，
满有行奇事的大能：
它胜过撒但、罪与死，
并在患难最暗之时鼓舞人。
六
但那些未重生的人
对它只知其声；
他们的热心与劳苦越多，
越是妄称他的名。
七
撒但讥诮他们徒然的尝试，
无论他们谈论、祷告或讲道；
只要对罪的爱尚存，
他的权能稳在他们所及之外。
八
但你们信徒可以喜乐，
撒但深知你们大能的朋友；
他因你们救主的声音战兢，
并承认他不能达到目的。

## 125 Paul’s Voyage / 125 保罗的航程

===Original===
By Newton
Acts 27.
I
If Paul in Cesar’s court must stand,
 

 He need not fear the sea,
 

 Secured from harm on every hand
 

 By the divine decree.
II
Although the ship in which he sail’d
 

 By dreadful storms was toss’d,
 

 The promise over all prevailed,
 

 And not a life was lost.
III
Jesus! the God whom Paul adored,
 

 Who saves in time of need;
 

 Was then confess’d by all on board,
 

 A present help indeed!
IV
Though neither sun nor stars were seen,
 

 Paul knew the Lord was near;
 

 And faith preserved his soul serene,
 

 When others shook for fear.
V
Believers thus are toss’d about,
 

 On life’s tempestuous main;
 

 But grace assures beyond a doubt,
 

 They shall their port attain.
VI
They must, they shall appear one day
 

 Before their Saviour’s throne;
 

 The storms they meet with by the way
 

 But make his power known.
VII
Their passage lies across the brink
 

 Of many a threat’ning wave;
 

 The world expects to see them sink,
 

 But Jesus lives to save.
VIII
Lord, though we are but feeble worms,
 

 Yet since thy word is past,
 

 We’ll venture through a thousand storms,
 

 To see thy face at last.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
使徒行传 27。
一
保罗既必须站在凯撒的法庭，
他无需惧怕大海，
得保守稳脱各方的伤害，
借着神圣的谕令。
二
虽然他所乘的船
被可怕的风暴抛掷，
应许胜过一切，
没有一条性命丧亡。
三
耶稣！保罗所敬拜的神，
在需要之时施行拯救；
那时被船上众人承认，
确是随时的帮助！
四
虽然不见太阳星辰，
保罗知主在近旁；
信心保守他的灵魂平静，
当别人因惧怕战抖。
五
信徒这样被抛掷，
在人生风暴的洋面；
但恩典确保无疑，
他们必到达口岸。
六
他们必须、也必要有一日
出现在他们救主的宝座前；
他们路上所遇的风暴
只使人认识他的大能。
七
他们的航程横过
许多威吓之浪的边缘；
世界指望见他们沉没，
但耶稣活着施行拯救。
八
主啊，我们虽只是软弱的虫，
但你的话既已发出，
我们愿冒险穿过千场风暴，
最终得见你的面。

## 126 The Good That I Would Do, I Do Not / 126 我所愿意的善，我反不作

===Original===
By Newton
Romans 7:19.
I
I would, but cannot sing﻿—
 

 Guilt has untuned my voice,
 

 The serpent sin’s envenom’d sting
 

 Has poison’d all my joys.
II
I know the Lord is nigh,
 

 And would, but cannot pray;
 

 For Satan meets me when I try,
 

 And frights my soul away.
III
I would, but can’t repent,
 

 Though I endeavour oft;
 

 This stony heart can ne’er relent
 

 Till Jesus make it soft.
IV
I would, but cannot love,
 

 Though woo’d by love divine:
 

 No arguments have power to move
 

 A soul so base as mine.
V
I would, but cannot rest
 

 In God’s most holy will;
 

 I know what he appoints is best,
 

 Yet murmur at it still.
VI
O could I but believe!
 

 Then all would easy be;
 

 I would, but cannot﻿—Lord, relieve;
 

 My help must come from thee!
VII
But if indeed I would,
 

 Though I can nothing do;
 

 Yet the desire is something good,
 

 For which my praise is due.
VIII
By nature prone to ill,
 

 Till thine appointed hour,
 

 I was as destitute of will,
 

 As now I am of power.
IX
Wilt thou not crown at length
 

 The work thou hast begun,
 

 And with a will afford me strength
 

 In all thy ways to run?
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
罗马书 7:19。
一
我愿歌唱，却不能——
罪咎使我的嗓音走了调，
那蛇罪的有毒螫刺
毒害了我一切的喜乐。
二
我知道主在近旁，
我愿祷告，却不能；
因撒但在我尝试时拦截，
把我的灵魂吓退。
三
我愿悔改，却不能，
虽然我屡屡努力；
这石头的心永不能软化，
直到耶稣使它柔软。
四
我愿爱，却不能，
虽然被神圣之爱恳求；
没有论据有能力感动
像我这样卑贱的灵魂。
五
我愿安息，却不能，
在神最圣洁的旨意中；
我知道他所定的是最好的，
却仍对它发怨言。
六
哦，我若能相信！
那样一切都容易；
我愿意，却不能——主啊，求你解救；
我的帮助必从你而来！
七
但我若真愿意，
虽然我一无所能；
那愿望已是某种良善，
为此赞美归于我。
八
按本性倾向恶，
直到你所定的时刻，
我从前如何缺乏能力，
也一样缺乏意愿。
九
你岂不肯至终加冕
你已动工的工作，
并赐我一个意愿、供我力量
奔跑你的一切道路？

## 127 Salvation Drawing Nearer / 127 救恩越来越近

===Original===
By Newton
Romans 13:11﻿–﻿12.
I
Darkness overspreads us here,
 

 But the night wears fast away;
 

 Jacob’s Star will soon appear,
 

 Leading on eternal day!
 

 Now ’tis time to rouse from sleep,
 

 Trim our lamps, and stand prepared,
 

 For our Lord strict watch to keep,
 

 Lest he find us off our guard.
II
Let his people courage take,
 

 Bear with a submissive mind
 

 All they suffer for his sake﻿—
 

 Rich amends they soon will find:
 

 He will wipe away their tears,
 

 Near himself appoint their lot;
 

 All their sorrows, pains, and fears,
 

 Quickly then will be forgot.
III
Though already saved by grace,
 

 From the hour we first believed;
 

 Yet while sin and war have place,
 

 We have but a part received:
 

 Still we for salvation wait,
 

 Every hour it nearer comes!
 

 Death will break the prison gate
 

 And admit us to our homes.
IV
Sinners, what can you expect,
 

 You who now the Saviour dare,
 

 Break his laws, his grace reject?
 

 You must stand before his bar!
 

 Tremble, lest he say, depart!
 

 Oh! the horrors of that sound!
 

 Lord, make every careless heart
 

 Seek thee while thou may’st be found.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
罗马书 13:11–12。
一
黑暗在此遮蔽我们，
但黑夜速速过去；
雅各之星不久必显现，
引领永恒的白日！
现在是时候从睡中醒起，
整理我们的灯，站立预备，
为我们的主严守守望，
免得他发现我们不备。
二
愿他的子民壮胆，
以顺服的心忍受
一切为他所受的苦——
他们不久必寻得丰富的补偿：
他必擦去他们的眼泪，
在自己近旁派定他们的分；
他们一切的忧伤、疼痛与恐惧，
那时必速速被忘却。
三
虽然已靠恩典得救，
从我们初信之时；
但罪与争战尚存之时，
我们只领受了一部分：
我们仍等候救恩，
它每时更近！
死亡必打开监狱之门，
接我们进入我们的家。
四
罪人们，你们能指望什么，
你们如今敢对抗救主，
违犯他的律法，拒绝他的恩典？
你们必站在他的台前！
战兢吧，免得他说：离去！
哦！那声音的恐怖！
主啊，求你使每个疏忽的心
趁你可寻找时寻求你。

## 128 That Rock Was Christ / 128 那磐石就是基督

===Original===
By Newton
1 Corinthians 10:4.
I
When Isr’el’s tribes were parch’d with thirst,
 

 Forth from the rock the waters burst,
 

 And all their future journey through
 

 Yielded them drink and gospel too?
II
In Moses’ rod a type they saw
 

 Of His severe and fiery law;
 

 The smitten rock prefigured Him
 

 From whose pierced side all blessings stream.
III
But ah! the types were all too faint
 

 His sorrows or His worth to paint;
 

 Slight was the stroke of Moses’ rod,
 

 But He endured the wrath of God.
IV
Their outward rock could feel no pain,
 

 But ours was wounded, torn, and slain;
 

 The rock gave but a wat’ry flood,
 

 But Jesus pour’d forth streams of blood.
V
The earth is like their wilderness,
 

 A land of drought and sore distress,
 

 Without one stream from pole to pole
 

 To satify a thirsty soul.
VI
But let the Saviour’s praise resound;
 

 In him refreshing streams are found,
 

 Which pardon, strength, and comfort give,
 

 And thirsty sinners drink and live.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
哥林多前书 10:4。
一
当以色列的众支派干渴，
水从磐石迸出，
并在他们后来的全程，
供给他们饮品与福音？
二
他们在摩西的杖中看见
他严厉火焰律法的预表；
那被击打的磐石预表他，
一切福分从他刺穿的肋旁流出。
三
但啊！这些预表都太微弱，
不足以描绘他的忧伤或他的宝贵；
摩西杖的击打轻微，
但他忍受了神的忿怒。
四
他们外面的磐石不感疼痛，
但我们的被击伤、撕裂、杀害；
那磐石只流出水洪，
但耶稣倾倒出血的溪流。
五
这地像他们的旷野，
一片干旱与极度困苦之地，
从这极到那极无一溪流
能满足干渴的灵魂。
六
但愿救主的赞美回响；
在他里面寻得更新的溪流，
它们赐下赦免、力量与安慰，
干渴的罪人喝了就活。

## 129 My Grace Is Sufficient for Thee / 129 我的恩典够你用的

===Original===
By Newton
2 Corinthians 12:9.
I
Oppress’d with unbelief and sin,
 

 Fightings without, and fears within,
 

 While earth and hell, with force combined.
 

 Assault and terrify my mind;
II
What strength have I against such foes,
 

 Such hosts and legions to oppose?
 

 Alas! I tremble, faint, and fall;
 

 Lord, save me, or I give up all.
III
Thus sorely press’d, I sought the Lord,
 

 To give me some sweet cheering word;
 

 Again I sought, and yet again;
 

 I waited long, but not in vain.
IV
Oh! ’twas a cheering word indeed!
 

 Exactly suited to my need;
 

 “Sufficient for thee is my grace,
 

 Thy weakness my great power displays.”
V
Now I despond and mourn no more,
 

 I welcome all I fear’d before:
 

 Though weak, I’m strong, though troubled, blest,
 

 For Christ’s own power shall on me rest.
VI
My grace would soon exhausted be,
 

 But his is boundless as the sea;
 

 Then let me boast with holy Paul,
 

 That I am nothing, Christ is all.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
哥林多后书 12:9。
一
被不信与罪压迫，
外有争战，内有惧怕，
当地和地狱联合势力
攻击恐吓我的心；
二
我对这样的仇敌有何力量，
怎敌这样的军旅与军团？
唉！我战兢、昏厥、跌倒；
主啊，救我，不然我放弃一切。
三
这样被重压，我寻求主，
求他给我一句甘甜鼓舞的话；
我再次寻求，又一次；
我长久等候，却不徒然。
四
哦！那确是一句鼓舞的话！
恰合我的需要；
「我的恩典够你用的，
你的软弱彰显我的大能。」
五
如今我不再沮丧忧伤，
我欢迎先前所怕的一切：
虽软弱，却刚强；虽受扰，却有福，
因基督自己的大能必安息在我身上。
六
我的恩典不久必枯竭，
但他的如海无边；
那么让我与圣洁的保罗同夸：
我是一无所是，基督是一切。

## 130 The Inward Warfare / 130 里面的争战

===Original===
By Newton
Galatians 5:17.
I
Strange and mysterious is my life,
 

 What opposites I feel within!
 

 A stable peace, a constant strife;
 

 The rule of grace, the power of sin:
 

 Too often I am captive led,
 

 Yet daily triumph in my Head.
II
I prize the privilege of prayer,
 

 But oh! what backwardness to pray!
 

 Though on the Lord I cast my care,
 

 I feel its burden every day;
 

 I seek his will in all I do,
 

 Yet find my own is working too.
III
I call the promises my own,
 

 And prize them more than mines of gold;
 

 Yet though their sweetness I have known,
 

 They leave me unimpress’d and cold:
 

 One hour upon the truth I feed,
 

 The next I know not what to read.
IV
I love the holy day of rest,
 

 When Jesus meets his gather’d saints;
 

 Sweet day, of all the week the best!
 

 For its return my spirit pants:
 

 Yet often through my unbelief,
 

 It proves a day of guilt and grief.
V
While on the Saviour I rely,
 

 I know my foes shall lose their aim;
 

 And therefore dare their power defy,
 

 Assured of conquest through his name:
 

 But soon my confidence is slain,
 

 And all my fears return again.
VI
Thus diff’rent powers within me strive,
 

 And grace and sin by turns prevail;
 

 I grieve, rejoice, decline, revive,
 

 And vict’ry hangs in doubtful scale:
 

 But Jesus has his promise past,
 

 That grace shall overcome at last.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
加拉太书 5:17。
一
我的生命奇特而奥秘，
我里面感受何等的对立！
稳定的平安，持续的争战；
恩典的掌权，罪的权势：
我屡屡被掳，
却每日在我元首里夸胜。
二
我珍视祷告的特权，
但哦！何等退后不愿祷告！
我虽把挂虑卸给主，
却每日感受它的重担；
我在一切所行中寻求他的旨意，
却发现我自己的也在运行。
三
我称应许为我自己的，
珍视它们过于金矿；
虽然我知道它们的甘甜，
它们却使我无感冷淡：
一时我以真理为粮，
下一时我不知该读什么。
四
我爱那圣洁的安息日，
当耶稣与聚集的圣徒相会；
甘甜的日子，一周之最！
我的灵切盼它的再来：
然而常因我的不信，
它证明是罪咎与忧伤之日。
五
当我倚靠救主，
我知我的仇敌必失其目标；
因此敢藐视他们的权势，
确信借他的名得胜：
但不久我的自信被杀，
我一切的恐惧又回来。
六
这样不同的能力在我里面争战，
恩典与罪轮流得势；
我忧伤、喜乐、衰退、复苏，
胜负悬于难定的天平：
但耶稣已发出他的应许，
恩典终必得胜。

## 131 Contentment.17 / 131 知足.17

===Original===
By Cowper
Philippians 4:11.
I
Fierce passions discompose the mind,
 

 As tempests vex the sea;
 

 But calm content and peace we find
 

 When, Lord, we turn to thee.
II
In vain by reason and by rule
 

 We try to bend the will;
 

 For none but in the Saviour’s school
 

 Can learn the heavenly skill.
III
Since at his feet my soul has sat,
 

 His gracious words to hear;
 

 Contented with my present state,
 

 I cast on him my care.
IV
“Art thou a sinner, soul?” he said,
 

 “Then how canst thou complain?
 

 How light thy troubles here, if weigh’d
 

 With everlasting pain?
V
If thou of murm’ring wouldst be cured,
 

 Compare thy griefs with mine;
 

 Think what my love for thee endured,
 

 And thou wilt not repine.
VI
’Tis I appoint thy daily lot,
 

 And I do all things well:
 

 Thou soon shalt leave this wretched spot,
 

 And rise with me to dwell.
VII
In life, my grace shall strength supply
 

 Proportion’d to thy day;
 

 At death, thou still shalt find me nigh,
 

 To wipe thy tears away.”
VIII
Thus I, who once my wretched days
 

 In vain repinings spent,
 

 Taught in my Saviour’s school of grace,
 

 Have learn’d to be content.
===Chinese===
库珀 作
腓立比书 4:11。
一
猛烈的情欲扰乱人心，
如风暴搅扰大海；
但我们寻得平静的知足与平安，
主啊，当我们转向你。
二
徒然凭理性和规条
我们试图折服意志；
因为惟独在救主的学校
才能学会这属天的技巧。
三
自从我的灵魂坐在他脚前，
听他恩慈的话；
我以现今的境况为足，
把挂虑卸给他。
四
「你是一个罪人吗，灵魂？」他说，
「那你怎能抱怨？
你的患难何等轻，若与
永远的疼痛相比？
五
你若愿得医治脱离怨言，
把你的忧伤与我的相比；
思想我的爱为你忍受了什么，
你就不再发怨言。
六
是我派定你每日的分，
我作一切都好：
你不久必离开这悲惨之地，
与我同升同住。
七
在生命，我的恩典必供力量，
与你的日子相称；
在死亡，你仍必发现我在近旁，
擦去你的眼泪。」
八
这样，我曾把悲惨的日子
枉费于怨言之中，
在救主恩典的学校受教，
学会了知足。

## 132 Old Testament Gospel / 132 旧约中的福音

===Original===
By Cowper
Hebrews 4:2.
I
Isr’el, in ancient days,
 

 Not only had a view
 

 Of Sinai in a blaze,
 

 But learn’d the gospel too:
 

 The types and figures were a glass
 

 In which they saw a Saviour’s face.
II
The paschal sacrifice,
 

 And blood be-sprinkled door,
 

 Seen with enlighten’d eyes,
 

 And once applied with power,
 

 Would teach the need of other blood
 

 To reconcile an angry God.
III
The Lamb, the Dove, set forth
 

 His perfect innocence,
 

 Whose blood of matchless worth
 

 Should be the soul’s defence:
 

 For He who can for sin atone
 

 Must have no failings of his own.
IV
The scape-goat on his head
 

 The people’s trespass bore,
 

 And to the desert led,
 

 Was to be seen no more:
 

 In him our Surety seem’d to say,
 

 “Behold! I bear your sins away.”
V
Dipt in his fellow’s blood,
 

 The living bird went free;
 

 The type, well understood,
 

 Express’d the sinner’s plea;
 

 Described a guilty soul enlarged,
 

 And by a Saviour’s death discharged.
VI
Jesus, I love to trace
 

 Throughout the sacred page
 

 The footsteps of thy grace,
 

 The same in every age!
 

 O grant that I may faithful be
 

 To clearer light vouchsafed to me!
===Chinese===
库珀 作
希伯来书 4:2。
一
以色列在古时，
不仅得以看见
燃烧的西奈，
也学了福音：
那些预表与形象是一面镜，
他们在其中看见救主的面。
二
逾越节的祭牲，
和洒血的门楣，
蒙光照的眼看，
一旦凭大能应用，
会教导需要别的血，
来与忿怒的神和好。
三
羔羊、鸽子，表明
他完全的无辜，
他那无比宝贵的血
当作灵魂的防御：
因为那能为罪赎罪的，
自己必须毫无过失。
四
替罪羊在头上
担当百姓的过犯，
被领到旷野，
不再被看见：
在他里面我们的中保似说：
「看哪！我担当你们的罪而去。」
五
蘸在同伴的血里，
那活鸟得以自由；
这预表，若被善解，
表达罪人的申诉；
描绘一个有罪的灵魂得释放，
借救主的死得开释。
六
耶稣，我爱循踪
你贯穿圣页的
恩典脚踪，
每个时代都一样！
哦，求你使我忠信于
所赐给我更清楚的光！

## 133 The Word Quick and Powerful / 133 道的活泼与有力

===Original===
By Newton
Hebrews 4:12﻿–﻿13.
I
The word of Christ our Lord,
 

 With whom we have to do,
 

 Is sharper than a two-edged sword
 

 To pierce the sinner through!
II
Swift as the lightning’s blaze
 

 When awful thunders roll,
 

 It fills the conscience with amaze,
 

 And penetrates the soul.
III
No heart can be conceal’d
 

 From his all-piercing eyes:
 

 Each thought and purpose stands reveal’d,
 

 Naked without disguise.
IV
He sees his people’s fears,
 

 He notes their mournful cry;
 

 He counts their sighs and falling tears,
 

 And helps them from on high.
V
Though feeble is their good,
 

 It has its kind regard;
 

 Yea, all they would do, if they could,
 

 Shall find a sure reward.
VI
He sees the wicked too,
 

 And will repay them soon,
 

 For all the evil deeds they do,
 

 And all they would have done.
VII
Since all our secret ways
 

 Are mark’d and known by thee,
 

 Afford us, Lord, thy light of grace,
 

 That we ourselves may see.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
希伯来书 4:12–13。
一
基督我们主的道，
我们必须与他相交，
它比两刃的剑更快，
能把罪人刺透！
二
快如闪电的光芒，
当可怕的雷霆滚动，
它使良心充满惊愕，
并穿透灵魂。
三
没有心能隐藏
避开他全然穿透的眼：
每个思想和意念站立显露，
赤露而无遮掩。
四
他看见他子民的恐惧，
他记念他们哀恸的呼声；
他数算他们的叹息和落泪，
并从高处帮助。
五
虽然他们的善微弱，
它仍蒙恩慈的看顾；
是的，他们所愿作的一切（若能作），
必寻得确实的赏赐。
六
他也看见恶人，
并必速速报应，
为他们所行的一切恶，
并他们原本要作的一切。
七
既然我们一切隐秘的路
都被你记念知晓，
主啊，供备你恩典之光，
使我们能看见自己。

## 134 Looking Unto Jesus / 134 仰望耶稣

===Original===
By Newton
Hebrews 12:2.
I
By various maxims, forms, and rules,
 

 That pass for wisdom in the schools,
 

 I strove my passion to restrain;
 

 But all my efforts proved in vain.
II
But since the Saviour I have known,
 

 My rules are all reduced to one,
 

 To keep my Lord by faith in view;
 

 This strength supplies and motives too.
III
I see him lead a suff’ring life,
 

 Patient amidst reproach and strife;
 

 And from his pattern courage take
 

 To bear and suffer for his sake.
IV
Upon the cross I see him bleed,
 

 And by the sight from guilt am freed;
 

 This sight destroys the life of sin,
 

 And quickens heav’nly life within.
V
To look to Jesus as he rose,
 

 Confirms my faith, disarms my foes;
 

 Satan I shame and overcome,
 

 By pointing to my Saviour’s tomb.
VI
Exalted on his glorious throne,
 

 I see him make my cause his own;
 

 Then all my anxious cares subside,
 

 For Jesus lives, and will provide.
VII
I see him look with pity down,
 

 And hold in view the conq’ror’s crown.
 

 If press’d with griefs and cares before,
 

 My soul revives, nor asks for more.
VIII
By faith I see the hour at hand,
 

 When in his presence I shall stand;
 

 Then it will be my endless bliss
 

 To see him where and as he is.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
希伯来书 12:2。
一
凭各样的格言、形式与规条，
那些在学校被当作智慧的，
我试图约束我的情欲；
但我一切的努力归于徒然。
二
但自从我认识救主，
我的规条全归并为一条，
凭信心使我主常在眼前；
这供备力量，也供备动机。
三
我见他过受苦的一生，
在辱骂与争竞中忍耐；
从他的榜样得勇气，
为他忍受并受苦。
四
在十字架上我见他流血，
借这景象脱离罪咎；
这景象治死罪的生命，
并在里面苏醒属天的生命。
五
仰望复活的耶稣，
坚立我的信心，解除我的仇敌；
我使撒但羞愧并胜过他，
借指向我救主的坟墓。
六
他被高举在荣耀的宝座上，
我见他认我的事业为他自己的；
那我一切焦虑的挂虑平息，
因耶稣活着，必预备。
七
我见他怜悯地向下观看，
把得胜者的冠冕持在眼前；
若先前被忧伤挂虑所压，
我的灵便复苏，不再多求。
八
凭信心我见那时刻临近，
当我将站在他面前；
那必是我无尽的福乐，
在他所在之处、照他本相见他。

## 135 Love Tokens / 135 爱的记号

===Original===
By Newton
Hebrews 12:5﻿–﻿11.
I
Afflictions do not come alone,
 

 A voice attends the rod;
 

 By both he to his saints is known,
 

 A Father and a God.
II
“Let not my children slight the stroke
 

 I for chastisement send,
 

 Nor faint beneath my kind rebuke,
 

 For still I am their Friend.
III
“The wicked I perhaps may leave
 

 Awhile, and not reprove;
 

 But all the children I receive,
 

 I scourge, because I love.
IV
“If therefore you were left without
 

 This needful discipline,
 

 You might with cause admit a doubt
 

 If you indeed were mine.
V
“Shall earthly parents then expect
 

 Their children to submit?
 

 And will not you, when I correct,
 

 Be humbled at my feet?
VI
“To please themselves they oft chastise,
 

 And put their sons to pain;
 

 But you are precious in my eyes,
 

 And shall not smart in vain.
VII
“I see your hearts at present fill’d
 

 With grief and deep distress;
 

 But soon these bitter seeds shall yield
 

 The fruits of righteousness.”
VIII
Break through the clouds, dear Lord, and shine!
 

 Let us perceive thee nigh!
 

 And to each mourning child of thine
 

 These gracious words apply.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
希伯来书 12:5–11。
一
患难不是独自来临，
有声音伴随这杖；
借这两样他向圣徒被认识，
是一位父亲和一位神。
二
「愿我的孩子不轻看
我为管教所发的击打，
也不在我恩慈的责备下丧胆，
因我仍是他们的朋友。
三
「恶人我或许任凭
一时，不加责备；
但我所收纳的一切孩子，
我必管教，因为我爱。
四
「你若被留下
没有这必需的管教，
你就有理由怀疑
你是否真是我的。
五
「地上的父母尚且盼望
他们的孩子顺服？
你们岂不更当在我管教时，
俯伏在我脚前谦卑？
六
「他们常为取悦自己而管教，
使他们的儿子受苦；
但你们在我眼中为宝，
所受的苦必不徒然。
七
「我见你们的心如今充满
忧伤与深重的困苦；
但这些苦种不久必结出
义的果子。」
八
亲爱的主，求你突破云层照耀！
让我们感知你在近旁！
并向你每个哀恸的孩子
应用这些恩慈的话。

## 136 Ephesus / 136 以弗所

===Original===
By Newton
Revelation 2:1﻿–﻿7.
I
Thus saith the Lord to Ephesus,
 

 And thus he speaks to some of us﻿—
 

 “Amidst my churches, lo, I stand,
 

 And hold the pastors in my hand.
II
“Thy works to me are fully known,
 

 Thy patience and thy toil I own;
 

 Thy views of gospel truth are clear,
 

 Nor canst thou other doctrine bear.
III
“Yet I must blame while I approve;
 

 Where is thy first, thy fervent love?
 

 Dost thou forget my love to thee,
 

 That thine is grown so faint to me?
IV
“Recall to mind the happy days
 

 When thou wast fill’d with joy and praise;
 

 Repent﻿—thy former works renew﻿—
 

 Then I’ll restore thy comforts too.
V
“Return at once, when I reprove,
 

 Lest I thy candlestick remove;
 

 And thou too late thy loss lament:
 

 I warn before I strike﻿—Repent.”
VI
Hearken to what the Spirit saith,
 

 “To him that overcomes by faith,
 

 The fruit of life’s unfading tree
 

 In paradise his food shall be.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
启示录 2:1–7。
一
主对以弗所这样说，
他也这样对我们中的一些人说——
「看哪，我站在我众教会中间，
把众牧者握在我手中。
二
「你的工作我全然知晓，
你的忍耐与劳碌我承认；
你对福音真理的看见清晰，
也不能容忍别的教义。
三
「但我赞许之余必须责备；
你起初火热的爱在哪里？
你岂忘记了我对你的爱，
以致你对我的爱这样冷淡？
四
「追想那快乐的日子，
当你充满喜乐与赞美；
悔改——重续你从前的工作——
我也必恢复你的安慰。
五
「我一责备，你立时回转，
免得我挪移你的烛台；
免得你太迟哀恸所失：
我击打之前先警告——悔改。」
六
要听圣灵所说的话：
「对那凭信心得胜的人，
生命不凋之树的果子，
在乐园中必作他的食物。」

## 137 Smyrna / 137 士每拿

===Original===
By Newton
Revelation 2:11.
I
The message first to Smyrna sent,
 

 A message full of grace,
 

 To all the Saviour’s flock is meant
 

 In every age and place.
II
Thus to his church, his chosen bride,
 

 Saith the great First and Last,
 

 Who ever lives, though once he died,
 

 “Hold thy profession fast.
III
“Thy works and sorrow well I know,
 

 Perform’d and borne for me;
 

 Poor though thou art, despised and low,
 

 Yet who is rich like thee?
IV
“I know thy foes, and what they say,
 

 How long they have blasphemed;
 

 The synagogue of Satan they,
 

 Though they would Jews be deem’d.
V
“Though Satan for a season rage,
 

 And prisons be your lot,
 

 I am your Friend, and I engage
 

 You shall not be forgot.
VI
“Be faithful unto death, nor fear
 

 A few short days of strife;
 

 Behold! the prize you soon shall wear,
 

 A crown of endless life!”
VII
Hear what the Holy Spirit saith,
 

 Of all who overcome;
 

 “They shall escape the second death,
 

 The sinner’s awful doom!”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
启示录 2:11。
一
那首先送到士每拿的信息，
一个满有恩典的信息，
是对救主羊群中一切人的，
在每个时代每个地方。
二
这样对他的教会、他拣选的新妇，
那伟大的首先的和末后的说，
他虽曾死却永远活着：
「要持守你的表白坚定。
三
「你的工作与忧伤我深知，
是为我行、为我忍受；
你虽贫穷、被轻看、卑微，
但谁像你这样富足？
四
「我知道你的仇敌和他们的谈论，
他们亵渎了多久；
他们是撒但的会堂，
虽然他们想被当作犹太人。
五
「虽然撒但一时狂怒，
监狱成为你们的分，
我是你的朋友，我承担保证，
你们必不被忘记。
六
「要至死忠信，也不要怕
少数短日的争斗；
看哪！你们不久要戴的奖赏，
一顶无尽生命的冠冕！」
七
要听圣灵论一切得胜者的话；
「他们必逃脱第二次的死，
那罪人可怕的定命！」

## 138 Sardis / 138 撒狄

===Original===
By Cowper
Revelation 1﻿–﻿6.
I
“Write to Sardis,” (saith the Lord,)
 

 And write what he declares,
 

 He whose Spirit and whose word
 

 Upholds the seven stars:
 

 “All thy works and ways I search,
 

 Find thy zeal and love decay’d;
 

 Thou art call’d a living church,
 

 But thou art cold and dead.
II
“Watch, remember, seek, and strive,
 

 Exert thy former pains:
 

 Let thy timely care revive,
 

 And strengthen what remains:
 

 Cleanse thine heart, thy works amend,
 

 Former times to mind recall,
 

 Lest my sudden stroke descend,
 

 And smite thee once for all.
III
“Yet I number now in thee
 

 A few that are upright;
 

 These my Father’s face shall see,
 

 And walk with me in white:
 

 When in judgment I appear,
 

 They for mine shall be confess’d,
 

 Let my faithful servants hear,
 

 And woe be to the rest!”
===Chinese===
库珀 作
启示录 1–6。
一
「要写信给撒狄，」（主说，）
并写他所宣告的，
他的灵与他的话
扶持那七星：
「我鉴察你一切的工作与道路，
发现你的热心与爱衰残；
你被称为活的教会，
但你又冷又死。
二
「要儆醒、记念、寻求并力争，
重新尽你从前的殷勤：
愿你及时的关心复苏，
并坚固那所存留的：
洁净你的心，改善你的行为，
追想从前的时日，
免得我突然的打击降临，
一次把你击杀。
三
「然而我如今在你里面数点
少数正直的人；
他们必见我父的面，
并穿白衣与我同行：
当我在审判中显现，
他们必被承认属我；
愿我忠信的仆人听见，
其余的人有祸了！」

## 139 Philadelphia / 139 非拉铁非

===Original===
By Newton
Revelation 3:7﻿–﻿13.
I
Thus saith the Holy One and true,
 

 To his beloved faithful few,
 

 “Of heaven and hell I hold the keys,
 

 To shut or open as I please.
II
“I know thy works, and I approve,
 

 Though small thy strength, sincere thy love;
 

 Go on, my word and name to own,
 

 For none shall rob thee of thy crown.
III
“Before thee see my mercy’s door
 

 Stands open wide to shut no more;
 

 Fear not temptation’s fiery day,
 

 For I will be thy strength and stay.
IV
“Thou hast my promise, hold it fast,
 

 The trying hour will soon be past;
 

 Rejoice, for lo! I quickly come
 

 To take thee to my heav’nly home.
V
“A pillar there no more to move,
 

 Inscribed with all my names of love,
 

 A monument of mighty grace,
 

 Thou shalt for ever have a place.”
VI
Such is the conqueror’s reward,
 

 Prepared and promised by the Lord!
 

 Let him that hath the ear of faith,
 

 Attend to what the Spirit saith.
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
启示录 3:7–13。
一
那圣洁真实者对他所爱
忠信的少数这样说：
「我持天国与地狱的钥匙，
随我旨意关闭或开启。
二
「我知道你的工作，我也称许，
虽然你力量微小，你的爱真诚；
继续承认我的道和名，
因为无人能夺去你的冠冕。
三
「看，我怜悯的门在你前
敞开，不再关闭；
不要怕试探的火日，
因为我必作你的力量与倚靠。
四
「你有我的应许，当持守牢固，
试炼之时不久必过；
喜乐吧，看哪！我速速再来，
接你到我天上的家。
五
「作一根不再移动的柱子，
刻上我一切爱的名，
作大能恩典的纪念碑，
你必永远有位置。」
六
这就是得胜者的赏赐，
由主预备并应许！
愿有信心之耳的，
留意圣灵所说的话。

## 140 Laodicea / 140 老底嘉

===Original===
By Newton
Revelation 3:14﻿–﻿20.
I
Hear what the Lord, the great Amen,
 

 The true and faithful Witness, says;
 

 He form’d the vast creation’s plan,
 

 And searches all our hearts and ways.
II
To some he speaks as once of old,
 

 “I know thee, thy profession’s vain;
 

 Since thou art neither hot nor cold.,
 

 I’ll spit thee from me with disdain.
III
“Thou boasted, ‘I am wise and rich,
 

 Increased in goods and nothing need;’
 

 And dost not know thou art a wretch,
 

 Naked and poor and blind and dead.
IV
“Yet while I thus rebuke I love,
 

 My message is in mercy sent;
 

 That thou may’st my compassion prove,
 

 I can forgive if thou repent.
V
“Wouldst thou be truly rich and wise!
 

 Come, buy my gold in fire well tried,
 

 My ointment to anoint thine eyes,
 

 My robe thy nakedness to hide.
VI
“See, at thy door I stand and knock!
 

 Poor sinner, shall I wait in vain?
 

 Quickly thy stubborn heart unlock,
 

 That I may enter with my train.
VII
“Thou canst not entertain a king,
 

 Unworthy thou of such a guest!
 

 But I my own provisions bring,
 

 To make thy soul a heavenly feast.”
===Chinese===
牛顿 作
启示录 3:14–20。
一
要听主、那伟大的阿们、
真实信实的见证者所说的；
他拟定浩瀚受造界的计划，
并鉴察我们一切的心与路。
二
他对一些人说话，如古时一样：
「我知道你，你的表白徒然；
你既不冷也不热，
我必不屑地把你吐出。
三
「你自夸说：我是富足，已经发了财，
一样都不缺；
却不知道你是那可怜、
赤身、贫穷、瞎眼、死的。
四
「然而我责备之时仍爱，
我的信息是凭怜悯差来；
你可以证明我的同情，
你若悔改，我能赦免。
五
「你愿真富足、真有智慧吗！
来，向我买火炼的金子，
买我的膏油膏你的眼，
买我的衣裳遮你的赤身。
六
「看，我站在你门外叩门！
可怜的罪人，我岂要徒然等候？
快快开你顽固的心门，
使我带同随从进入。
七
「你不能款待一位君王，
你不配这样的宾客！
但我自带筵席，
要使你的灵魂成为天上的盛筵。」

## 141 The Little Book.18 / 141 小书卷.18

===Original===
By Newton
Revelation 10.
I
When the beloved disciple took
 

 The angel’s little open book,
 

 Which by the Lord’s command he eat,
 

 It tasted bitter after sweet.
II
Thus when the gospel is embraced,
 

 At first ’tis sweeter to the taste
 

 Than honey or the honey-comb,
 

 But there’s a bitterness to come.
III
What sweetness does the promise yield,
 

 When by the Spirit’s power seal’d!
 

 The longing soul is fill’d with good,
 

 Nor feels a wish for other food.
IV
By these inviting tastes allured,
 

 We pass to what must be endured;
 

 For soon we find it is decreed,
 

 That bitter must to sweet succeed.
V
When sin revives and shows its power,
 

 When Satan threatens to devour,
 

 When God afflicts, and men revile,
 

 We draw our steps with pain and toil.
VI
When thus deserted, tempest-toss’d,
 

 The sense of former sweetness lost,
 

 We tremble lest we were deceived
 

 In thinking that we once believed.
VII
The Lord first makes the sweetness known,
 

 To win and fix us for his own;
 

 And though we now some bitter meet,
 

 We hope for everlasting sweet.

===Chinese===
牛顿 作
启示录 10。
一
当那蒙爱的门徒接过
天使手中展开的小书，
奉主命把它吃了，
先甜后苦的味道。
二
这样，当福音被接受，
起初它比蜜
或蜜房更甜，
但随后有苦味来到。
三
应许何等甘甜，
当蒙圣灵的大能盖印！
渴慕的灵魂充满美善，
不再想望别的食物。
四
被这些引人的滋味吸引，
我们进入必须忍受的；
因我们不久发现已经命定，
苦必随甜而来。
五
当罪复苏显出权势，
当撒但威胁要吞吃，
当神击打、人辱骂，
我们拖着步伐带着疼痛劳苦。
六
当这样被撇弃、被风暴颠簸，
从前甘甜的感受失落，
我们战兢，惟恐受了欺骗，
以为我们曾一度相信。
七
主先使我们认识甘甜，
为赢得我们、固定我们属他；
虽然我们如今遇见些苦，
我们盼望永远的甘甜。
